728. ELIZA PRICE was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, one watch, value 6 l.; one chain, value 2 l.; three seals, value 35 s.; one key, value 5 s.; one handkerchief, value 2 s.; 10 s., in monies numbered, and one 15 l. bank note, the property of George Page, from his person; and WILLIAM HAMERTON was indicted for feloniously receiving, harbouring, and maintaining the said Eliza Price, knowing her to have committed the said felony.
GEORGE PAGE. I am an attorney. On the 1st of March I dined at a coffee-house in the Strand, about four o'clock - I was alone and very much intoxicated. After I left I remember being in Long-acre, in my way home to my dwelling-house, at Camden-town. About this time I was so overcome that I lost all recollection. When I came to myself, I found I was in Tottenham-court-road, and missed all this property; I have never seen it since. The 15 l. note was No. 10479, dated December 29, 1818.
JOHN NETTLEFOLD. I am an ironmonger. On the 3d or 4th of March, I gave the prisoner, Hamerton, change for a 15 l. note.
GEORGE DYER. I produce the note from the Bank.
WILLIAM GODFREY. I am an officer. About half-past six o'clock I apprehended Hamerton.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-229]
729. THOMAS DELLER was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of April, six live pigs, price 30 s., the property of the Reverend John Bransby.
REV. JOHN BRANSBY. I live at Stoke Newington, and keep pigs in my farm-yard; On the 13th of April, I saw them safe. Next morning, I missed three - the yard has a fence six feet high; I have never seen them since. On Friday morning the other three were taken; I had seen them safe about four o'clock on Thursday morning. My servant went to town and brought them back the same evening.
WILLIAM BAILEY. I am a gardener, and live at Stoke Newington. On Friday morning, about five o'clock, I heard the pigs making a noise; I got out of bed, and went to my window, which commands a view of the prosecutor's yard. In a little while I saw a man get out of one of the styes; he had a small bag, which appeared to be full. He got over the fence, which is six feet high, and then over another fence into the road - I then lost sight of him. I dressed myself and pursued him, but lost him.
Cross-examined by MR. NORRIS. It was not a very tall man.
THOMAS UPJOHN. I am a poultry salesman, and live in Leadenhall-market. On Friday, the 16th of April, about half-past six o'clock in the morning, the prisoner brought me three pigs, which he asked me 18 s. for. I suspected him, took him in custody, and gave him in charge. The prosecutor claimed the pigs, and I delivered them to his servant.
BARNARD GLEED. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in charge.
REV. JOHN BRANSBY. I know the pigs to be mine.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a young man, who asked me to sell them.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-230]
730. ELIZABETH M'KENZIE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, one bolster, value 4 s.; two pillows, value 6 s.; two blankets, value 5 s.; one sheet, value 3 s.; two saucepans, value 2 s.; one pail, value 1 s., and one looking-glass, value 3 s., the goods of Sarah Thomas, in a lodging-room, let to the prisoner, to be used with the lodgings.
SARAH THOMAS. I am a widow, and live in New-court, Goswell-street. I let the lodgings to the prisoner's husband, not to her.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-231]
731. MILES M'CABE and DANIEL CRANE were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, six sheets, value 30 s., the property of Charles Stanbridge; and EDWARD CRANE was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, he well knowing them to have been stolen.
MARY SEARLE. I am servant to Mr. Charles Stanbridge, who lives at Camden-town. On the 15th of March, between seven and eight o'clock in the evening, I missed the sheets off the railing at the back of the house, where they hung to dry. The persons must have climbed over a high wall to get them. They were safe an hour before. I saw nobody in the yard. The officer brought them to me about a fortnight after, and I knew them to be our's - the marks were taken off. Only four were found - I lost six.
JOHN DAVIS. I am a patrol of Bow-street. In consequence of information which I received, I went to the house of the witness Leslie, and found in the kitchen, where she lives, two pair of sheets. It was at No. 3, Little Ogle-street. This was a fortnight after the 15th of March, Searle saw them, and claimed them.
Cross-examined. Q. What relation is Leslie to the prisoners - A. She is sister to the Cranes.
CHARLOTTE LESLIE. This witness appeared in Court, but refused to be sworn, and was committed.
HENRY HOWARD. I am watch-house-keeper of St. Pancras. I was in company with Davis, when we foundthe four sheets in the same kitchen, where we found Charlotte Leslie.
MARY ANN WILSON. I live at No. 10, Southampton-place, Camden-town. I am a single woman. I know the three prisoners, and I know Charlotte Leslie.
Q. Did you ever see the prisoners at her house - A. No, I did not see them.
Q. You are sworn to tell the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth, did you ever see these sheets - A. No, never.
Q. What were you bound over to prove - A. I really do not know. I was examined at Bow-street.
Q. What did you prove there - A. I really do not know.
Q. What did you swear there - A. I saw a bundle come up one evening.
Q. Do not prevaricate, I must hear all you know; are you any relation of either of the prisoners - A. No.
Q. Did either of them live at No. 10, Southampton-place - A. No.
Q. Do you know them - A. Yes.
Q. Do you know them all three - A. Yes; I have known them all three from children.
Q. Now you know what you have said on a former occasion, where did Edward Crane live - A. At No. 2, Southampton-place.
Q. Were you with him about a fortnight before the prisoners were taken up - A. No, I was not.
Q. Were you ever in his company on a Monday - A. Yes.
Q. How long before he was taken up - A. I cannot say.
Q. When were you at Bow-street, the 1st of April, was it not - A. I do not know.
Q. How long before you were at Bow-street, were you in company with Edward Crane at Southampton-place - A. I do not think I had been in his place for a fortnight or three weeks.
Q. What day of the week was it - A. I cannot recollect.
Q. Mind that you are on your oath - A. I am, and would not wish to take a false oath.
Q. Whatever day it was, were you alone with him when you first went to No. 2, Southampton-place - A. No, I was not alone.
Q. At what time of the day did you go there - A. About five or six o'clock in the evening.
Q. Was he alone then - A. Yes.
Q. Did any other persons come into your company while you was with him - A. No.
Q. Before you left him did any others come in - A. Miles M'Cabe came in.
Q. Anybody else - A. No, I did not see anybody else.
Q. Did anybody else come in - A. I do not recollect.
Q. Do you swear that - A. Yes.
Q. Then nobody else came in - A. Not for a great while after M'Cabe came.
Q. Did anybody else come in before Miles M'Cabe left A. No, I really cannot recollect.
Q. While you was with Edward Crane, did anybody else come in - A. I cannot recollect.
Q. Did M'Cabe bring any thing - A. He brought a bundle.
Q. Was it large or small - A. Very small.
Q. What did it contain - A. I do not know; I did not look at it.
Q. Did it contain linen, or boxes, or iron, or what - A. I do not know.
Q. Was it dry or wet - A. I do not know; I did not take notice.
Q. How long was it in your sight - A. While I was there.
Q. How long were you there - A. Only a quarter of an hour.
Q. You was there only a quarter of an hour after Miles M'Cabe came in - A. No, only a quarter of an hour.
Q. During that quarter of an hour that you was there, I ask you, on your oath, did anybody else come into the same room with you and M'Cabe while you was there - A. I really cannot say.
Q. You are called upon as a witness to speak the truth, I now ask you if anybody else came in - A. I really cannot say.
Q. Did you tell the truth when you was at Bow-street - A. I do not know.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-232]
732. MILES M'CABE and DANIEL CRANE were again indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, one tablecloth, value 5 s., the goods of Robert Beattie.
ANN BEATTIE. I am the wife of Robert Beattie, who lives at No. 1, Pratt-street, Camden-town. On the 5th of March, about half-past three o'clock in the afternoon, I lost this table-cloth out of the garden at the back of the house, which is fenced by railing four feet high; I saw it safe ten minutes before. About three weeks after, the officer brought it - the prisoners were in custody.
THOMAS WADMORE. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Tottenham-court-road with my brother. On the 8th of March, the table-cloth was pledged with me by Mary Ann Wilson, who was examined in the last case.
JOHN DAVIS. Mary Ann Wilson took me to the shop where the table-cloth was pledged, after the prisoners were taken up.
ROBERT BEATTIE. I was coming out of my house a little after three o'clock in the afternoon, and saw a young man inside my garden railing; one or two of the railings were broken - I made him come out - the linen was hanging up to dry. There was another man standing by the railing - they both went away. I do not know them.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-233]
733. JOHN MURPHY was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, one coat, value 14 s., and one handkerchief, value 1 s., the goods of Joseph Sayer.
JOSEPH SAYER. I live in Old Gravel-lane, and am carman to Messrs. Sharp and Co. On the 29th of March, in the afternoon, I missed my coat out of the yard; it was adark snuff coloured coat. My fellow-servant lost another coat.
ROBERT REDMAN. I am a carman. I was near the stable, and saw the prisoner come out of the yard with two coats on his shoulder. He ran by me into Old Gravel-lane.
ELIZA LITTLE. I was at work opposite the stable, and saw the prisoner go into the yard, and come out with two coats over his shoulder - I am sure he is the man. He had no coat when he went in.
WILLIAM SIMMS. I am a constable. I apprehended the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Whipped, and Discharged.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-234]
734. HENRY ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of February, one sheet, value 6 s., the goods of John Radley.
JOHN RADLEY. I am a gardener, and live at Bow. On the 26th of February, I was in my garden, and heard some persons call out, that a man had robbed me; I turned round and saw the prisoner running. He threw the sheet down, and I collared him. It hung on a line at the door.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

735. SARAH SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of March, 2 lbs. of flock, value 6 d.; two blankets, value 6 s.; one mattress-case, value 2 s., and one rug, value 2 s., the goods of John Morris, in a lodging-room.
ELIZA MORRIS. I am the wife of John Morris, who keeps the Old Crown, public-house, Webb-square, Shoreditch. I let the prisoner a two-pair front-room, furnished, at 3 s. 6 d. a week - she came on the 18th of March. Next morning, I saw her come down with a bundle under her arm; she told my husband on the evening of the 18th, that she wanted to go out early in the morning, and I showed her how to open the door. Next day, about five o'clock I looked through a hole in the door, and saw the flock laying about the room. About seven o'clock my husband went up stairs, intending to go into the room; she said every thing was right, but the things were dirty. I went in with the constable, and missed the property stated in the indictment. The bed was emptied, mattress, and tick and all were sold - they were let to her with the lodgings.
SARAH ACKELEY. I am a broker, and live in Club-row, Spitalfields. On the 17th of March, I bought the bed-stick and bolster of the prisoner, for 2 s. 6 d. Next day I bought a mattress-tick and 2 lbs. of flock for 1 s. 6 d., I also bought two blankets of her. She said that she lived in Webb-square,
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The woman said I should have them back again for a few halfpence more.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-236]

MARGARET HILL. I am the wife of Peter Hill, who lives at Bromley. On the 12th of March, I saw a man take my petticoat off the line, and run off. The prisoner was brought back in about ten minutes, and gave it to me.
EDWARD HEGAN. I heard the alarm, and secured the prisoner with the petticoat under his coat.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I bought it for 4 d.
GUILTY. Aged 46.

737. MARY ANN JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of March, nine towels, value 5 s., and two petticoats, value 3 s., the goods of Sarah Taylor, widow.
SARAH TAYLOR. I am a widow, and live in Charles-street, Hatton-garden. On the 9th of March, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon, these things hung in my parlour to dry; the door was a little ajar - I was backward. The prisoner pushed the screen aside, and my daughter said there was a woman; I saw the prisoner going out; she said she wanted Mrs. Gregory - I said she did not live there. My daughter said she had got all the things under her arm - she said they were Mrs. Gregory's things. I pulled her into the room, and took them from under her arm - she had got to the door.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I asked for Mrs. Cotterill.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

738. CHARLES HOLLOWAY was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, one saw, value 8 s., the goods of Samuel Scales; one saw, value 8 s., and one rule, value 3 s., the goods of William Brazier.
JOHN TAYLOR. I am a carpenter, and live at Heston; the prisoner was a gentleman's servant. On the 5th of April, at night, my shutters were broken open, and these tools taken - Scales and Brazier work at my shop. The staple was drawn, and every thing taken out of my shop. I suppose it was done about two o'clock in the night, as I heard a noise about that time, and got up, but saw nothing.
SAMUEL SCALES. I lost my tools out of the shop - they were safe the night before.
WILLIAM BRAZIER. I lost my tools the same night.
RICHARD RAVENER. I am a constable. On the 6th of April, I took the prisoner, at the Coach and Horses, at Hanwell, and found the tools upon him. He offered them all to me for sale for 10 s.; he sold them to a carpenter in the room for 7 s. - I secured him. He said his name was Hiscock, which I knew to be false.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I bought them for 6 s.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-239]
739. JOHN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, one musket, value 7 s., the goods of William Haggett.
WILLIAM HAGGETT. I am a labourer, and live in Essex-court, Park-street, Borough. On the 24th of February, between ten and eleven o'clock in the morning, I missed my musket out of the passage. I went after the prisoner, and took him at Ratcliff-cross, between twelve and one o'clock that day, with the musket on his arm. I was informed he had gone that way, pursued him, and took him. I am certain he is the man.
JOSEPH ADAMS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in charge on the 24th of February, with the musket. He made his escape from the office, and was taken again. I am sure he is the man.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
- I am an officer. I was bringing the prisoner to Newgate; he broke from me and ran down Newgate-market - I lost him, but retook him again about fifteen days after, and am sure he is the man. He had the marks of the handcuffs on his wrist that he ran away with.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-240]
740. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of April, two coats, value 5 l., the property of George Smith.
SUSAN SMITH. I am the daughter of George Smith, who is a labourer, and lives at Hackney. I was crossing the road, and saw the prisoner with two coats under his arm. I ran in, and missed my father's coats from behind the door; I pursued him, calling Stop thief! and a gentleman stopped him. I am certain he is the man.
SUSAN POWELL. The prisoner ran by me with the coats under his arm; the last witness called Stop thief! he jumped into the hedge of a garden, left the coats there, and returned without them. The constable stopped him. I never lost sight of him.
THOMAS GOODWIN. I am a constable. I stopped the prisoner, who was running in Grove-street. He said there was nothing amiss, it was only a joke; I took him back and found the coats in the hedge.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a man run into the garden with a bundle, and pursued him, but lost him.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-241]
741. MICHAEL GARATTY was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of April, three yards and a half of velveteen, value 9 s., the goods of George William Rainbow, from his person.
GEORGE WILLIAM RAINBOW. I am a tailor. On the 22d of April, about half-past two o'clock in the day, I was behind a coach, in the Commercial-road. In about a minute the prisoner and another man came up - the prisoner sat by my side. In a little time he took my bundle from under my arm; I told him to let it alone - he revised to give it to me, and said it was a joke. He struck me several blows about the head, and gave the bundle to his companion, who ran away with it. I got down and pursued him; he then turned back, and gave it to the prisoner on the coach; they knocked me down, and struck me several blows. I took it from them, and took the prisoner into custody.
Cross-examined by MR. LOVET. Q. There were a great many people in the road at the time - A. A great many. The prisoner continued sitting on the coach. He did not ask me to make room for his companion.
WILLIAM PARSONS. I was passing, and saw a man give the bundle to the prisoner, who was riding behind the coach - the prisoner knocked the prosecutor down. I stopped to look at them, and took the prisoner to the office. I believe he was the worse for liquor.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been drinking; the prosecutor grumbled at my coming behind the coach, and knocked my hat off; I took his parcel and threw it after my hat, another man came, and took both hat and bundle.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-242]

743. DANIEL MAHONEY was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of April, one handkerchief, value 15 d., the goods of Henry Beverly Wakeling, from his person.
HENRY BEVERLY WAKELING. On the 23d of April, about eight o'clock in the evening, I was in Drury-lane with Mr. Young; the prisoner and another were following us very close. I felt a hand in my pocket, turned round and collared the prisoner - his companion ran away. My handkerchief was not found; I had it a moment before. I am certain I caught the prisoner's hand in my pocket.
JOHN YOUNG. I was with Wakeling; he said he had lost his handkerchief, and seized the prisoner - the other ran away. Nobody else had been near us.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

745. JOHN MALBOROUGH was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, two coats, value 4 s., and one spencer, value 6 d., the goods of John Butterworth; and one coat, value 5 s., the goods of William Hayman.
ANN BUTTERWORTH. I am the wife of John Butterworth, who is a weigher in the Custom House. On the 16th of March, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning, the coats were in the butler's pantry, at a gentleman's house which I have the care of, in Marlborough-street. My little girl called me; I ran and saw the prisoner in the passage with tracts in his hand, which he wanted to sell. I sent him away, and told him he should have knocked at the door; I bolted the door, and saw him with the coats, on the area steps. I pursued him, and secured him in the street with them - one belongs to William Hayman, and the other is my husband's.
MARY ANN GOSS. I heard the last witness call out Stop thief! and assisted in securing the prisoner.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

746. THOMAS POWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, two spoons, value 4 s. 9 d., and 12 s. in copper monies numbered, the property of Michael Brown.
MICHAEL BROWN. I am a tailor, and live in James-street. On the 14th of March, about four o'clock, I went out with my wife - I returned about five, found my parlour window open, and the canary bird taken away, with two spoons, and 12 s. in copper. I saw the prisoner in the passage calling to the lodgers - he was apprehended next morning. I found my spoons at a silversmith's in Cheapside - I knew the prisoner before.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner at a public-house on Saffron-hill. I told him I took him for stealing the spoons - he said he sold them in Cheapside, and told me where to find the bird.
JAMES STEVENS. I am clerk to Mr. Bull, who is a silversmith, and lives in Cheapside. I bought two spoons of a boy about the age of the prisoner, for 4 s. 9 d. I did not ask him any questions.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

748. CHARLES THOMPSON was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of April, 56 lbs. of mutton, value 30 s., the property of William Robbins.
WILLIAM ROBBINS. I am a butcher, and live in High-street, Shadwell. On the 24th of April, about a quarter before five o'clock in the morning, a young man, named James Stevens, knocked at my door, and informed me that my place was broken open. I ran down, found the door open, and missed a carcase of mutton - it was double-jointed all round, which was very remarkable - I never saw one like it before. I afterwards found the whole carcase cut up very badly, and can swear to it. I fitted the joints to it.
ROBERT HICKS. I am a patrol of Old Gravel-lane. On the 24th of April, about a quarter after four o'clock in the morning, I found the prisoner laying down in a twine-shed, with his head on a bag. I asked him what he was doing there? he said he had laid down to sleep. I asked him what he had got in his bag? he said it was some rags. I found there were two fore-quarters and four other pieces of mutton - he said he found it in the West India Docks.
JOHN PATERSON. I am a watchman. About a quarter after four o'clock in the morning of the 24th of April, Hicks brought the prisoner to me. I went into the shed, and found the hind part of the mutton hanging up about four yards from where he laid.
WILLIAM STEVENS. On the 24th of April, about a quarter before five o'clock in the morning, I was looking through the window, and saw the prosecutor's door open; I went down, found the staple had been drawn, and gave the alarm.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. I am a constable. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house. I found two kidnies and a large spike nail on him, and two knives, one in each stocking - I also found some fat in a bag in his hat. The prosecutor saw the mutton, and compared it with one of the knuckles - it corresponded exactly; it was double-jointed.
GUILTY. Aged 36.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-249]
749. DANIEL AUSTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, two candlesticks, value 2 s., the goods of Thomas Eagles.
SARAH EAGLES. I am the wife of Thomas Eagles; we keep a chandler's shop. I went out to speak to a little boy, and on coming back I saw the prisoner in the shop, and asked him what he wanted? he asked me if I wanted to buy a cheese-knife? I said No. When he was gone, I missed a pair of candlesticks off the counter. I ran out, and overtook him - he gave them to me instantly. He appeared in distress.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

750. MARY KELLY was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, one 5 l. bank note, the property of James Chubb, from his person.
JAMES CHUBB. I am a harness-maker, and live at Holloway. On the 15th of March, about eleven o'clock, I was at the Lord Nelson, public-house, Shoreditch, the prisoner and two others were there - they asked me to give them a glass of gin. I offered the landlady a 5 l. note to pay for it - the prisoner snatched it out of my hand, put it down towards her pocket-hole, and would not deliver it up. The watchman took her - it was not found. She said if I would go to her house she would give me the note.
MARGARET HOARE. I keep the Lord Nelson. I was in the bar, and saw the prisoner snatch the note out of the prosecutor's hand.
ANN M'DERMOT. I was with the prisoner. She snatched the note out of the prosecutor's hand. He treated me with a glass of gin.
Prisoner's Defence. I met him in the City-road - he took me to the house. I never saw the note.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-251]

756. GEORGE SCONE was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of April, one handkerchief, value 1 s., the goods of Henry James Brooks, from his person.
HENRY JAMES BROOKS. On the 27th of April, about ten o'clock at night, I was in Holborn with John Mullins, who said a person was picking my pocket - I felt, and missed my handkerchief. I turned round, and saw the prisoner throw something white away, but am not certain that it was my handkerchief. I collared him, and called Watch! he struck me three times, and ran off, but was stopped. My handkerchief was not found.
Cross-examined by MR. ALLEY. Q. Did you not charge him with an assault - A. No.
JOHN MULLINS. I was with Brooks, and saw the prisoner behind him. I suspected him, and informed the prosecutor - he never collared the prisoner. I went back to look for the handkerchief, but it was gone.
FRANCIS GAMMAGE. I am a watchman. I heard the alarm, and stopped the prisoner. He said it was only a row.
Prisoner's Defence. The prosecutor collared me - I pushed him off, he struck me, and I struck him.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-257]

759. THOMAS HARRIS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of April, one handkerchief, value 2 s., from the person of a man whose name is unknown.
THOMAS STEVENS. I am a potatoe salesman in Covent-garden. On the 17th of April, at half-past eleven o'clock at night, I was taking my scales and weights to the public-house, and saw the prisoner and another young man rush out of the door as I went in; as I returned to Covent-garden I saw them again about twenty yards from the door, the prisoner took a handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket, turned back again, and went into the public-house - I followed him, and stood by the door; he came out again with the same young man - they went up the street, and both stood against a railing in the street. I passed them, three gentlemen and a lady came by, returning from the Theatre, they both followed them, and I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the gentleman's pocket - it was a blue and white one. I pointed him out to the watchman. I am certain he is the man.
Q. Why did you not stop the gentleman, and tell him - A. Because I was once robbed myself, and when I took the man I was surrounded by a gang of about twenty, and got almost killed.
CHARLES HUMPHRIES. On the 17th of April I was with Bond under the Piazza, and met a watchman, who pointed the prisoner out to me in a public-house, I beckoned him out, searched him, and found a blue and white handkerchief in his hat; he said he had had it almost a fortnight - it smelt very strong of perfume; he said it was not marked, but it was. Bond found another handkerchief on him.
WILLIAM BOND. I searched the prisoner, and found a handkerchief in his side-pocket; he said it was not marked, but it was.
Prisoner's Defence. They are both my own.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-260]
760. ROBERT ASHBY was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of March, one ham, value 6 s., the property of John Charles Lea.
JAMES PERCIVAL. I am servant to John Charles Lea, who is a cheesemonger, at Hackney. I missed a ham from the door, ran out, and found the prisoner with it under his jacket.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 62.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-261]
761. DAVID MORGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of February, three blankets, value 15 s.; two sheets, value 6 s.; one table-cloth, value 2 s.; one apron, value 6 d., and one waistcoat, value 1 s., the goods of Philip Mark.
PHILLIP MARK. I am a tailor, and live in High Holborn; the prisoner was my porter, and his wife was my housekeeper. On the 11th of March I missed some property, and had them both searched. I found a pocketbook on his wife containing twenty-four duplicates. The prisoner said he pledged the articles, and gave his wife the duplicates.
WILLIAM THISSLETON. I am a constable. The prisoner and his wife were given into my charge. I found the duplicates of the property in the pocket-book.
THOMAS WINDSOR ALLEN. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Clare-street. The prisoner pledged three blankets, two sheets, a table-cloth, an apron, and a waistcoat, with me, at different times.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 57.
Confined One Year.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-262]
762. LAWRENCE PERRIN was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, one handkerchief, value 18 d., the property of Thomas Clark, from his person.
THOMAS CLARK. I live in Bernard's Inn, Holborn. Between eight and nine o'clock in the evening, I was in Drury-lane; I was called and missed my handkerchief - it was produced to me.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a constable. I was in Wych-street, and saw the prisoner following the prosecutor, who had a gentleman with him. I watched him, and saw him pull the handkerchief out a little way; he turned round to look about him - I was on the opposite side of the way. He then took it quite out; Forbes, who was with me secured him, and took it out of his bosom. I informed the prosecutor.
JOHN FORBES. I am a constable. I was with Thompson, and saw the prisoner following the prosecutor; I crossed over, and saw him take the handkerchief partly out; he then turned round looked behind him, and took it quite out - he put it in his bosom. I laid hold of him, and took it out of his bosom.
CHARLES JONES. I was with Forbes, and saw the handkerchief taken from the prisoner's bosom.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I picked it up.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-263]
763. JOHN MAYWOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, one wheelbarrow, value 15 s., the goods of William Mobbs.
WILLIAM MOBBS. I lost my wheelbarrow, but know nothing about it. Freeman who does is not here.
NOT GUILTY.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-264]
764. SARAH LONG was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of April, one coat, value 2 s., and one waistcoat, value 6 d., the goods of James Elliott.
ALICE ELLIOTT. I am the wife of James Elliott; we live in Granby's-buildings, Drury-lane. On the 25th of April, I lost this coat and waistcoat from the kitchen; next morning the prisoner passed at the bottom of the court; I secured her, and charged her with stealing them. She denied it.
ANN CRAWLEY. I saw the prisoner come out of the prosecutor's door with the coat. I am sure she is the woman.
LEWIS SAMUEL. I am a salesman, and live in Drury-lane. I bought the coat and waistcoat of the prisoner.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-265]
765. JOHN HURLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of April, one stewpan, value 10 s., the goods of Peter Markland.
PETER MARKLAND. I live in Green-street, St. George's in the East; the prisoner lived next door to me. On the 24th of April, I lost this stewpan out of an outhouse. On the Monday he was taken, and it was produced to me.
SAMUEL WILLIAMS. I am a seaman. On the 24th of April, I saw the prisoner in the prosecutor's yard, about seven o'clock in the morning; he took the stewpan, and put it over into his own yard - I live opposite, and am certain he is the man.
JOHN SMITHERS. I am a carman. I bought the pan of a boy for 4 s.
JOHN BROWN. I apprehended the prisoner, concealed between his bed and mattress.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-266]
766. BENJAMIN FIRBY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of April, eight yards of oil-cloth, value 20 s., the goods of John Harrell.
JOHN HARRELL. I live in East-street, Marylebone, and am a broker. I lost my oil-cloth, ran out and secured the prisoner with it, about four hundred yards off. He said a man gave it to him to carry.
WILLIAM BAILEY. I live opposite Harrell. I saw another man take the oil-cloth, and give it to a man; the prisoner was secured.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A man gave it to me to carry.
NOT GUILTY.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18190421-267]
767. JOHN ANNS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of April, one handkerchief, value 5 s., the goods of Thomas Dugard, from his person,
THOMAS DUGARD. I am a goldsmith, and live in Red-Lion-street, Clerkenwell. On the 25th of April, about one o'clock I was crossing from Sadler's Wells into St. John-street-road, a man called to me that my pocket had been picked; I found my handkerchief on the ground, and the prisoner close by me. I stopped him as he was pointed out to me; he said he knew nothing about it - nobody else was near me.
HENRY GREEN. I am a carpenter, and live in Hermitage-place,Sadler's Wells. I saw the prisoner walking behind the prosecutor in the road - I saw him put his hand into Mr. Dugard's pocket, and pull the handkerchief out. I called out to the prosecutor - he immediately dropped it. The prosecutor stopped him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18190421-268]
768. SARAH AFFLECK was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of February, one tea-kettle, value 18 d., the goods of William Thomas.
WILLIAM THOMAS. I live at Mutton-hill, and am a tool-broker. I missed a tea-kettle from my door, ran out, and secured the prisoner with it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
